OMP THIRD-PARTY NOTICES
================================

This file is a self-contained release notice payload.

LICENSE SCOPE
-------------

Unless a package says otherwise, first-party OMP code is licensed under
the MIT license in the companion LICENSE file, copyright 2026 Stencil Labs,
Inc.

This aggregate preserves notices and license texts; it does not replace,
restrict, expand, or supersede the terms of any applicable license. A component
listed as review-required is excluded from the project's first-party license
unless and until its provenance and permission are established.

REVIEWED NON-STANDARD RUST LICENSE BRANCHES
--------------------------------------------

cargo-deny keeps these approvals package-and-version scoped. New packages with
these licenses, unknown licenses, restricted/source-available licenses, and
unapproved copyleft licenses fail closed.

MPL-2.0 (file-level copyleft):
- bitmaps 2.1.0: https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/bitmaps/2.1.0/download
- im 15.1.0: https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/im/15.1.0/download
- option-ext 0.2.0: https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/option-ext/0.2.0/download
- sized-chunks 0.6.5: https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/sized-chunks/0.6.5/download

The exact Source Code Form availability requirement is: when Covered Software
is distributed in Source Code Form, MPL-2.0 section 3.1 requires the Covered
Software, including modifications, to be made available under MPL-2.0 and
recipients to be informed how to obtain a copy. When Covered Software is
distributed in Executable Form, section 3.2 requires its Source Code Form also
to be made available as section 3.1 describes, and recipients to be told how
to obtain it by reasonable means in a timely manner, at a charge no more than
the cost of distribution. The locked upstream Source Code Form is available at
the exact archive URLs above. The full MPL-2.0 text appears in the generated
Rust dependency section below.

Other explicitly reviewed cases:
- terminfo 0.9.0: WTFPL
EXCLUDED MATERIAL REQUIRING RIGHTS REVIEW
-----------------------------------------

The 90 provider logo files used by the TUI login surface have no documented
source or license in this repository. They are excluded from the project MIT
license pending rights review. Their exact filenames, SHA-256 digests, sizes,
dimensions, and status are recorded in:
crates/tui/assets/login/provenance.json

The 21 recovered Cursor/Devin protocol schema files likewise have no license
grant established by their files or recorded repository history. They are
excluded from the project MIT license, and use or redistribution requires
rights review. Exact historical source paths, commits, digests, sizes, and
status are recorded in:
fixtures/llm-oracle/vendor-schemas/provenance.json
The tracked notice at fixtures/llm-oracle/vendor-schemas/NOTICE.txt is
concatenated below.

The OMP emoji JavaScript/data is first-party material and is not part of either
excluded inventory.

PACKAGE-SPECIFIC AND NATIVE NOTICES
-----------------------------------

The following tracked package notices are concatenated verbatim. In the source
tree their paths identify the affected package or vendored material. The
embedded-Python payload comes from crates/py/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt and
includes its component license texts; package NOTICE and *.NOTICE files retain
upstream attribution, source locations, exclusions, and review requirements.

omp-ar attribution notice
=========================

The RAR decompression structures in `src/rar/rar4_decoder.rs` and
`src/rar/rar5_decoder.rs` are adapted from unrar5j by Stephane Bury:

https://github.com/RealBurst/unrar5j
Copyright 2025 Stephane Bury

unrar5j and this adaptation are licensed under the Apache License, Version
2.0. The complete license terms are in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` in the
repository root. The Rust implementation has been modified from the Java
upstream for integration into omp-ar; this notice does not claim ownership
of the upstream work.

Third-party notices
===================

License information for CPython, native libraries statically linked from the
pinned python-build-standalone release, and pure-Python packages frozen into
omp-py binaries. Generated by omp-py's scripts/fetch-python.sh from the
fetched release metadata and license corpus — do not edit by hand.

Dynamic host libraries
----------------------
Libraries and frameworks that PYTHON.json marks as dynamic system dependencies
are supplied by the target operating system. They are not copied into the
python-build-standalone static library corpus and are intentionally excluded
from the bundled-component notices below.

A. HISTORY OF THE SOFTWARE
==========================

Python was created in the early 1990s by Guido van Rossum at Stichting
Mathematisch Centrum (CWI, see http://www.cwi.nl) in the Netherlands
as a successor of a language called ABC.  Guido remains Python's
principal author, although it includes many contributions from others.

In 1995, Guido continued his work on Python at the Corporation for
National Research Initiatives (CNRI, see http://www.cnri.reston.va.us)
in Reston, Virginia where he released several versions of the
software.

In May 2000, Guido and the Python core development team moved to
BeOpen.com to form the BeOpen PythonLabs team.  In October of the same
year, the PythonLabs team moved to Digital Creations, which became
Zope Corporation.  In 2001, the Python Software Foundation (PSF, see
https://www.python.org/psf/) was formed, a non-profit organization
created specifically to own Python-related Intellectual Property.
Zope Corporation was a sponsoring member of the PSF.

All Python releases are Open Source (see http://www.opensource.org for
the Open Source Definition).  Historically, most, but not all, Python
releases have also been GPL-compatible; the table below summarizes
the various releases.

    Release         Derived     Year        Owner       GPL-
                    from                                compatible? (1)

    0.9.0 thru 1.2              1991-1995   CWI         yes
    1.3 thru 1.5.2  1.2         1995-1999   CNRI        yes
    1.6             1.5.2       2000        CNRI        no
    2.0             1.6         2000        BeOpen.com  no
    1.6.1           1.6         2001        CNRI        yes (2)
    2.1             2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         no
    2.0.1           2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.1           2.1+2.0.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.2           2.1.1       2002        PSF         yes
    2.1.3           2.1.2       2002        PSF         yes
    2.2 and above   2.1.1       2001-now    PSF         yes

Footnotes:

(1) GPL-compatible doesn't mean that we're distributing Python under
    the GPL.  All Python licenses, unlike the GPL, let you distribute
    a modified version without making your changes open source.  The
    GPL-compatible licenses make it possible to combine Python with
    other software that is released under the GPL; the others don't.

(2) According to Richard Stallman, 1.6.1 is not GPL-compatible,
    because its license has a choice of law clause.  According to
    CNRI, however, Stallman's lawyer has told CNRI's lawyer that 1.6.1
    is "not incompatible" with the GPL.

Thanks to the many outside volunteers who have worked under Guido's
direction to make these releases possible.

----------------

http://www.math.sci.hiroshima-u.ac.jp/~m-mat/MT/MT2002/emt19937ar.html. The following are
the verbatim comments from the original code::

   A C-program for MT19937, with initialization improved 2002/1/26.
   Coded by Takuji Nishimura and Makoto Matsumoto.

   Before using, initialize the state by using init_genrand(seed)
   or init_by_array(init_key, key_length).

   Copyright (C) 1997 - 2002, Makoto Matsumoto and Takuji Nishimura,
   All rights reserved.

python-build-standalone license corpus: licenses/LICENSE.sqlite.txt
-------------------------------------------------------------------
All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code form
or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial, and by
any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code has
been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of code can
be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have public
domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.

omp-walker provenance notice
============================

This package continues the earlier `pi-walker` implementation incorporated in
the OMP source tree. The implementation has since been renamed, extended, and
modified for the current workspace. The retained `pi-walker` references in
source comments, worker names, and performance-test descriptions document
that code lineage.

No separate third-party copyright or license notice for `pi-walker` is present
in the incorporated source snapshot. The current omp-walker package is
distributed under MIT; complete terms are in `LICENSE`.

SOURCE NOTICE: fixtures/llm-oracle/vendor-schemas/NOTICE.txt
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

VENDOR SCHEMA NOTICE

This directory contains 21 protocol schema files recovered from this repository's historical provider sources: one Cursor schema and twenty Devin schemas. The exact file-by-file source path, historical commit, digest, and size are recorded in provenance.json.

No license notice is present in any of these schema files, and the recorded repository history does not establish a license grant for them. Accordingly, these files are excluded from the project's root MIT license. Their use and redistribution require rights review. This notice does not assert ownership, origin beyond the factual history recorded in provenance.json, or permission from Cursor, Devin, or any other party.

provenance.json is the machine-readable inventory for this directory. Each .proto file must appear there exactly once with license_status "no_license_present", rights_review "required", and root_license_scope "excluded".

2.0 (the &quot;agreement&quot;). Data Provider(s) and Data Recipient(s) agree
as follows:

EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGES.

## 5. Definitions

5.1. &quot;Data&quot; means the material received by a Data Recipient under
this agreement.

5.2. &quot;Data Provider&quot; means any person who is the source of Data
provided under this agreement and in reliance on a Data Recipient&#x27;s
agreement to its terms.

5.3. &quot;Data Recipient&quot; means any person who receives Data directly
or indirectly from a Data Provider and agrees to the terms of this
agreement.

5.4. &quot;Results&quot; means any outcome obtained by computational analysis
of Data, including for example machine learning models and models&#x27;
